Every brush's box is now bounded at `MaxEditVoxels` (512 a side): past it the edit changes nothing, reports `refused`, and the script gets `scene.err.terrain_brush_too_large` -- the same run ends in a second. `fillFlat` is bounded in columns, `applyRules` in voxels; `voxelIndex` clamps before its cast and reads a NaN as 0; `net.request`'s timeout is held to a day before its conversion. Test: `terrain_tests.cpp`, "a brush no world has is refused whole, and fast". |
| D237 | audit, 2026-09-28 | A `BindToClose` handler that waited was never resumed while the game was paused, and the close loop spun for the whole grace period (audit A1) | fixed | Reproduced (`world_host_tests.cpp`, "a BindToClose handler that yields is waited for with the game paused"): with the world paused, `close(2.0)` spun for its two seconds and the handler's `task.wait(0.1)` never returned -- a paused world's `tick` does nothing, and the save, timer and preload waiters are resumed inside it. The close loop now unpauses the world it is closing, paces its ticks to the fixed step instead of spinning, hands HTTP results to their threads each tick, and -- for the game's own close, where there is a window -- pumps the window's events, so the system does not call a game saving on its way out unresponsive. The editor's Stop, save-all and open-scene paths go through the same loop. |
| D238 | audit, 2026-09-28 | One streamed cell that failed to load froze the simulation for good (audit A2) | fixed | Reproduced (`streaming_tests.cpp`, "a cell that failed does not hold the world waiting for it"): a cell of the minimum ring marked `Failed` left `minimumRingResident` false for ever, and the frame loop holds the simulation at zero ticks while it is false -- and a failed cell is never retried, by design. A failed cell now counts as settled, and its failure is logged once (`asset.err.stream_cell_failed`, naming the cell), so the world goes on without it and somebody is told why a piece is missing. |
| D239 | audit, 2026-09-28 | A join's ten-second timeout counted frames, and a join from the command line to a server not up yet became solo (audit A3) | fixed | Reproduced: a script's `Join` to a listener that never welcomes gave up after 602 frames -- two milliseconds of wall time with nothing pacing the frames, as in a minimised window, and four seconds at 144 Hz. The timeout is now seconds of wall time (`setJoinTimeout`, ten by default), read from a clock a test can replace; and a join from the command line, which dials again when a server goes away, also dials until one answers, rather than timing out into solo. Test: `network_session_tests.cpp`, "a join waits by the clock, not by frames". |
| D240 | audit, 2026-09-28 | A scene changed at run time did not stream, and the boot scene's cells went on streaming into it (audit A4) | fixed | Only the boot scene met the partition, in `WorldHost::boot`; `loadScene` read a new scene whole, and never told the streaming hosts, whose cells -- engine-made, so kept by `clearScene` -- went on streaming around the player, and a return to the boot scene brought its parts twice. The host now takes a `resetStreaming` from the engine, called before the old scene is cleared: `StreamingHost::reset` and `FieldStreamer::reset` evict what is resident as the pump would (a husk where a script holds a part), cancel reads and forget the index. The new scene then meets the same `partitionScene` the boot's did. A scene prepared in the background (`LoadSceneAsync`) was parsed whole and still loads whole. Test: `world_host_tests.cpp`, "a scene changed at run time is partitioned as the boot's was". |
| D241 | audit, 2026-09-28 | "Save everything" from play, and opening a scene from play, dropped the code typed into scene-script tabs during play (audit A5) | fixed | Found by reading, as the audit did: the editor's frame has no harness that drives these paths. The Stop button and the device-lost save wrote the tabs' text back into the world the stop restored; the save-all and open-scene paths stopped play without it, and a save then wrote the text from before play over what was on the screen. The write-back is now one function, `writeTypedSources` in `engine.cpp`, called on every way out of play that keeps the scene. |
| D242 | owner, 2026-09-28 | Each player's character flicked while walking in a real match, and each saw the other stutter (the multiplayer smoothness brief, causes 1 and 2) | fixed | Reproduced (`session_tests.cpp`, "a replica walking through jitter, reordering and loss is never corrected"): ten seconds of a held key over a transport holding each message 0 to 2 ticks, reordering some and losing two in a hundred, gave 276 corrections -- the authority applied whichever intent had arrived last, none on one tick and two on the next, and acknowledged the last received rather than the one its step came from. Now, protocol 22: an `Intent` carries its tick and the three before it; the authority queues them by tick and applies exactly one a tick, a few behind the newest (a delay that grows by a tick when the queue runs dry and shrinks after ten quiet seconds, changed only while the player is idle), the last one standing in for a tick that has not arrived; a snapshot acknowledges the tick whose step it holds; `GetIntent` reads the applied one. The replica keeps its prediction history when an answer names a tick it has no sample for, and a character that has just become its own is predicted only after it has taken the authority's state. The same run: zero corrections, and the authority's character steps exactly one tick of walk a tick. |
| D243 | agent, 2026-09-28 | A frame that ran no tick still ran a tick's work in replication: a server consumed input per frame, and a replica's clock ran at the frame rate (found fixing D242) | fixed | The frame loop calls `receive` on every frame that runs no tick, to keep the connection serviced; the replica advanced its server clock and interpolated there too, so a client drawing at 144 Hz ran that clock more than twice the tick rate and was snapped back at every snapshot -- other players' characters stepping instead of gliding -- and the authority's new input buffer would have applied an intent a frame. `receive` now says whether a tick follows (`ticking`), and only then are intents applied, limits counted, the clock advanced and remote parts interpolated. |
| D244 | owner, 2026-09-28 | A correction was a one-frame pop, and walking into loose parts corrected the character constantly (the multiplayer smoothness brief, causes 3 and 4) | fixed | A correction still moves the simulation at once, and the own character is now drawn with the difference as an offset that decays over about a tenth of a second (`VisualCorrection`, applied in `render::interpolatedCFrame`); past 2 m it is a teleport and drawn as one. Test: "a correction is drawn sliding over a tenth of a second". Measured with the owner's test game (a dedicated server and two headless clients on one machine, a virtual key driving one): open ground 0 corrections a second; through the loose crates up to 30 a second and 22 cm before, up to 19 a second and mostly 2 to 5 cm after the crates a replica only follows became passable from the side for its own character (`BodyDesc::passableForCharacters`, still standing on them from above); against a player standing still, none. What is left through the crates is the authority's character slowing as it pushes them. |
| D245 | owner, 2026-09-28 | Nothing showed how a connection was doing (the multiplayer smoothness brief, cause 5) | fixed | `ITransport::link` reports ENet's round trip, its variation and the share of packets lost; the snapshot carries the authority's view of that replica's input buffer (protocol 22). The F3 overlay has a Network panel -- ping, jitter, loss, snapshots and corrections a second, the last correction's size, the input buffer and how often it ran dry -- and `NetworkService:GetStats()` returns the same to a script. Test: `network_session_tests.cpp`, "a script reads how the connection is doing". |
| D246 | owner, 2026-09-28 | A dedicated server that could not open its port said so only in a console that closed with it (the multiplayer smoothness brief, note 2) | fixed | Reproduced with two copies of the exported server on one port: the second logged `net.err.transport_open_failed` and exited with code 1 -- the "keeps running" in the brief did not reproduce with this build -- but opened by a double click, its console closed with it and the reason with it. A dedicated server whose console is its own now shows the error in the system's message box before it exits. |
| D247 | audit, 2026-09-28 | Moving or renaming content rewrote the files naming it one by one with a truncating stream, and a file that failed was counted as naming nothing (audit A6) | fixed | The first reproduction tried a read-only file, which on Windows the stream still rewrote, so the test injects a writer that refuses the second of two files: before, the first was already rewritten and the second left pointing at the old path, silently. `retargetContentReferencesAll` now reads every file and works out every new text before writing any, writes each with `writeFileDurable`, and on the first file it cannot write puts back the ones it wrote and names it; the editor then moves or renames the content back and says which file, so no reference is left broken. Test: `content_tree_tests.cpp`, "a move whose references cannot all be rewritten leaves every file as it was". |
| D248 | audit, 2026-09-28 | Saving a streamed terrain wrote over a cell whose file would not read with only its loaded part, and deleted emptied cells before the index that no longer named them was written (audit A12) | fixed | Reproduced (`terrain_cells_tests.cpp`, "a cell whose file could not be read is not written over"): a cell named by the index and not loaded, with ground painted into its square and a file that would not read, was written anyway, and everything else its file held was lost. Such a cell is now left as it is on disk, its row kept, and the save reports that not everything was written. Emptied cells are listed rather than removed; their files go after the new index is written, durably, so a save that stops half way never leaves an index naming files that are gone. |
| D249 | audit, 2026-09-28 | The editor's smaller data losses: a device-lost "Save and restart" that ignored failed saves, an untitled scene under an open stamp dropped without a word, open shader tabs and terrain layer materials left at a moved file's old path, and documents saved without a flush (audit A5, A6, A12, the medium items) | fixed | Found by reading, as the audit did: the editor's frame has no harness for these paths. The device-lost save now collects what did not save and asks before restarting, naming the files; the unsaved-changes dialog says the untitled scene under an open stamp cannot be kept by Save; a move or rename retargets open content-file tabs (`followOpenFiles`) and each terrain's layer material URNs; the editor writes scenes, stamps, materials and scripts with `writeTextFileDurable`. |
| D250 | owner, 2026-09-28 | Jumping onto a block's corner in a match rolled the character back, sometimes again and again (ADR 0133's measurement) | fixed | Measured first (`network_session_tests.cpp`, "MEASURE a hundred jumps onto a block's corner", the real physics on both ends over jitter and loss): 2 corrections in 100 jumps with the physics replay, 29 in decaying runs without it. The causes found and fixed: a jump held for one tick whose intents all came after the authority had stood the previous tick in for it was never applied -- a press a late intent carries that its stand-in lacked is now applied at the next tick, late rather than lost (test: "a press whose intent came after its tick was stood in for is applied late, not lost"); a character that had just become the replica's own was taken at the answered tick without stepping again what was unanswered, and the first comparison corrected it by 27 cm -- a take now replays what is unanswered, as a correction does; the delay starts at 3 ticks; and the delay's growth while idle held a tick for ever once a peer stopped sending -- it now holds one tick for each new intent. Then **the replay became the live step** (ADR 0133): the replica remembers what it simulates -- its own character and every body it simulates -- after each tick's step (`PhysicsSync::remember`, through Jolt's per-body and per-character state), and a correction restores that island at the answered tick, puts the authority's word on the character in, and steps the whole physics again with the live step and the unanswered commands; a test holds the replay to the live walk bit for bit ("a replay from a remembered tick is the live step again, to the bit"). Found on the way: the steps taken again consumed the move a script had left for the next live step, which then moved nothing -- a step behind at every correction -- so what is pending is kept. The test ("a hundred jumps onto a block's corner over jitter and loss are not corrected"): no correction past a centimetre in the jumps, the one take at join (2.2 cm) aside; none at all without jitter. |
| D251 | owner, 2026-09-28 | Pushing a loose crate in a match corrected the character, and letting the character pass through crates from the side (D244) only made it rarer (ADR 0133, stage 2) | fixed | A crate the replica drew between snapshots was one its character met where the authority's crate had been ticks before. Now the loose parts within 8 m of the replica's own character, and what those touch, are simulated there: dynamic, solid, remembered with the character each tick, and corrected with it -- the authority's snapshot carries their velocities (protocol 23), a replay puts them where the authority had them, and the comparison counts a crate past a centimetre as a correction of the whole island. The side-pass stays for what is not predicted. Measured on the way (`network_session_tests.cpp`, "ten seconds pushing crates"): three corrections of 1 to 5 cm per run, clean or lossy, all at the moment two crates met; the two machines were found to step the island to the bit from the same state, and the misses came from differences of a fifth of a millimetre that the 1 cm threshold left alone -- so any difference over 10 µm is now stepped again without counting a correction. The test, at three seeds of loss and jitter and without: no correction past a centimetre while pushing, the character touching the crates and never inside one; and "a replica predicts the loose parts near its character and what they touch, and no further". |
| D252 | measurement, 2026-09-29 | Pushing the crates of the owner's test game, windowed and with a real key, was still corrected once in eleven seconds, by 4.8 cm: a replay with crates in it was not the live step (ADR 0133) | fixed | Reproduced headless with the same walk (the game's twelve crates, S and W held in turn): the replica agreed with the authority to 2 µm until the character met a crate's edge, drifted 0.4 mm, was stepped again from the authority's word -- and the replay came out 8 cm from it two ticks later. A test replaying every tick of a push, one step at a time, found why, one cause after another: putting the authority's word on a crate that already agreed woke it, and a crate asleep in the live step was stepped in the replay; its rotation went through a matrix and back; the controller put back in place found its contacts afresh at the crate's edge rather than keeping the ones of its own last step; and the solver warm-started from the contacts of the newest step rather than of the one restored. A replay now writes only what the authority disagrees about, nudges its character by a small correction without finding its contacts again, and the island keeps the solver's contact cache and whether each body was awake. Test: "a replay of a character pushing crates is the live step again, to the bit", fifty steps of a push compared bit for bit; the headless walk has no correction after the join. |
| D253 | measurement, 2026-09-29 | A frame landing exactly on a tick drew the next state, and the frame after it went back: at 120 Hz, every other frame, the world jumped ahead and back (ADR 0134) | fixed | Found by the rule test for the 2D layer: a `Part2D` at six metres a second, drawn at 120 Hz on the tick's clock, moved ten centimetres and then none rather than five and five. Alpha zero answered "the tick itself", as no history does, where it is the tick before the last -- the end the frame just short of a tick reached from the other side. The old test of it (`render_world_tests.cpp`, "a frame between two ticks is drawn between two states") held both ends to the newest tick and so held the jump in place. Alpha zero is now the tick before, and a world drawn at its tick -- headless, an editor holding it still -- passes no history. Test: "a 2D part at a steady speed moves the same distance every frame at 120 Hz". |
| D254 | owner, 2026-09-29 | The name over a character smeared when it walked in a match: nothing visual but the parts read where things are drawn (ADR 0134) | fixed | Traced to a pattern rather than one bug: `BillboardGui`, `SurfaceGui`, particles, the cameras of the three kinds of view, `Part2D`, `ProximityPrompt`'s box, the pointer's ray and the editor's pick all read the simulated transform, which moves a tick at a time, while the parts were drawn between ticks; and a correction's slide moved the corrected part alone, leaving its limbs and its name where the correction put it. Every visual consumer now reads `render::DrawPoses`, resolved once per instance per frame, whose slide carries everything under the corrected part; `tools/repo/drawcheck.luau` fails a visual file that reads a raw `cframe` or `worldCFrame`. Test: "what hangs on a moving part is drawn with it between ticks, and through a correction's slide" -- put back to the simulated place, the billboard alone was 15 pixels and 0.9 m off. |
| D255 | measurement, 2026-09-29 | Another machine's parts were never drawn between ticks: they stepped at 60 Hz on a replica (ADR 0134) | fixed | The frame loop took the history after the network's receive, and the receive is what moves another player's character to its new interpolated place -- so its previous place was already its current one. The history is now captured before the receive, and a correction of the own character moves its last captured place with it -- on a frame with a tick or without one -- in functions the frame loop and the tests both run (`app::runDrawnTick`, `app::receiveDrawn`). Test: "another machine's moving part is drawn between ticks, with what hangs on it" -- 4.17 cm every 144 Hz frame at six metres a second, no frame still. |
| D256 | audit, 2026-09-28 | Closing a dedicated server's console lost its saves: Windows ended the process before `BindToClose` ran (audit A7) | fixed | Reproduced with the real thing: a server whose `BindToClose` saves a slot, run in its own console and closed as the X closes it (`WM_CLOSE` to the console window) -- the package before this wrote no save, the build after it did. The console handler answered a close, a logoff or a shutdown at once, and Windows ends the process soon after whatever the answer; it now asks the engine to stop, says it has 3.5 s (`platform::stopDeadlineSeconds`), and waits up to 4.8 s for `platform::stopFinished`, which the engine calls once `host->close` has saved. Test: "a console closed waits for the engine to close, within the time Windows allows". |
| D257 | audit, 2026-09-28 | A `LoadScene` that failed left the game half torn down: a missing scene ran the open scene's close handlers and stayed, a malformed one was torn down before its parse failed (audit A8) | fixed | A scene is now found, read and parsed before anything closes; one that cannot be is refused with the open scene whole -- its close handlers not run, its scripts running -- and `SceneService.SceneLoadFailed(path, message)` says so. `loadScene` parses before it tears down, for every other caller. Test: "a LoadScene that cannot happen leaves the open scene as it was, and says so". |
| D258 | audit, 2026-09-28 | A scene change and a `Join` interleaved: a scene still in its close grace, or a prepared `LoadSceneAsync`, opened after the join, on a replica (audit A10) | fixed | Reproduced -- the test failed with the fix taken out, scene B opening on the replica after the join. `Join` now cancels every scene change this machine asked for (`WorldHost::cancelSceneChanges`: the queued one, the closing one and the prepared one, a waiting `LoadSceneAsync` told it failed), and `applyPendingScene` refuses on a replica. Test: "a Join during a scene change cancels the change, and no scene of its own opens on the replica". |
| D259 | audit, 2026-09-28 | A hot reload lost up to a second of save changes: the fresh host read the slots from disk before the outgoing one wrote them (audit A11) | fixed | Reproduced (the test fails without the fix): a slot changed just before the reload came back as it was a second earlier. The outgoing host now flushes its saves before the fresh one boots. Test: "a hot reload keeps what a script saved just before it". |
| D260 | audit, 2026-09-28 | Recovering a save from its backup destroyed the backup at the next write (audit F7) | fixed | Reproduced (the test fails without the fix: the slot was lost at the second damage). A main file that would not read is moved to `.corrupt` once its backup has been read in its place, so the next write finds no main to make the backup and the good backup stays. Test: "a slot read from its backup keeps that backup through the next save". |
| D261 | audit, 2026-09-28 | A stamp naming itself fanned out without a bound in width, re-reading and re-parsing its file at every placement (audit F6) | fixed | A load now builds at most `SceneIoReport::instanceLimit` instances (a million) before it stops placing stamps -- a stamp past it counted missing and kept, like a deleted one -- and reads and parses each stamp once (`StampLoad`). Test: "a stamp that names itself many times is read once, and stops at the load's limit": twenty children of itself, read once, stopped at the limit instead of 168,421 instances. |
| D262 | audit, 2026-09-28 | Image decode was unbounded and stb was built with every format (audit F8) | fixed | stb is built for PNG, JPEG and TGA only, with its dimension guard at 16384, and a header is checked before a pixel is allocated: past 8192 x 8192 is refused. The copy after the decode stays, bounded now by the same limit. Tests: "a header claiming a huge picture is refused before anything is allocated", "a format this engine does not use is not decoded". |
| D263 | audit, 2026-09-28 | Block size, voxel size and heights were checked as doubles and used as floats, so a positive number could become zero, a denormal or an infinity (audit F9) | fixed | `asset::saneFieldSettings` checks a field's settings as the floats they are -- a voxel from a centimetre to 64 m, heights within a million metres -- for a terrain cell file and a scene's cells alike, and a voxel world's block size is held to the same range from a scene, a partition and a script. Tests: "field settings are checked as the floats they are", "a block size that narrows to nothing is refused, from a file and from a script" (before, the file's `1e-300` became a block size of zero). |
| D264 | audit, 2026-09-28 | Crash diagnostics missed `abort()`, a throw on another thread and the C runtime's own checks, and each run's log replaced the last (audit A15) | fixed | On Windows `abort()` -- which is also how MSVC ends a thread whose exception nobody caught -- an invalid argument to the C runtime and a pure virtual call now each leave a dump and a note with a stack (`__fastfail` cannot be caught by design). The log keeps one generation (`engine.previous.log`), and a run that cannot write its log where it runs -- a game installed under Program Files -- writes it and its crash files to its own folder. The log stays beside the run otherwise, on purpose: that is where a person finds it. Test: the crash gate's three new modes, `abort`, `thread` and `invalid`, each required to leave a note naming it. |
| D265 | audit, 2026-09-28 | Every imported mesh drew its full detail at every distance: the level of detail read the translation from the wrong elements of a column-major matrix (audit R1) | fixed | Reproduced: the new test places an instance 200 m away through the renderer's own transform and failed with level 0. `selectMeshLod` reads the translation from `m[3]` and the axis lengths from the columns, and the fixture that put its offset in the same wrong element was corrected. The instanced reference image changed with it, on purpose: its far rows now draw coarser levels. Test: "a distant instance draws a coarser level, placed by the renderer's own transform (audit R1)". |
| D266 | audit, 2026-09-28 | With anti-aliasing off, the tonemap wrote into the window through a pipeline made for an RGBA8 target, where the swapchain is BGRA8 (audit R2) | fixed | Changed by reading: no validation layer on this machine reported it. A tonemap pipeline in the swapchain's format is made beside the plain one, and the graded look has its twin, as the FXAA pass already did. |
| D267 | audit, 2026-09-28 | A run with no display grew its memory by a gigabyte a second while the GPU fell behind (audit R8, P2) | fixed | Measured with the package: 11-ocean headless climbed to 2.3 GiB in four seconds and now holds at 168 MiB. `submitAndPresent` keeps the fence of each submitted frame and waits on the oldest past three in flight. |
| D268 | audit, 2026-09-28 | After 256 block images had ever been seen, a new block drew untextured for the rest of the run (audit R9) | fixed | Changed by reading. A full atlas now takes back the tile of an image no block shows any more, and the first image drawn into a new atlas is the only one that clears it -- before, the tile at index 0 did, which a reclaimed tile 0 would have wiped. |
| D269 | audit, 2026-09-28 | A surface shader whose pipelines the device refused showed magenta for one frame and then drew as the built-in surface without a word (audit R12) | fixed | Changed by reading. The failure is kept with the revision that caused it, so the material stays magenta until the source changes, and it is logged once (`render.warn.surface_pipeline_failed`). A recompile no longer waits for the GPU: a destroy releases once no frame holds the pipeline. |
| D270 | audit, 2026-09-28 | A renderer that failed to create because a skinned shader was missing left every shader it had made on the device (audit R14) | fixed | Changed by reading: that one refusal returned without `destroy`, which every other one calls. |
| D271 | audit, 2026-09-28 | Mining one block in a block world remeshed the 27 chunks round it and rebuilt up to 7 colliders -- the hitches of 14-voxels (audit P1) | fixed | Attributed with the new phase statistics (the slow frames were CPU drawing) and reproduced: the new test breaks a block inside a chunk and failed with 3 remeshes. A chunk's key digests its neighbours whole, and the mesh reads only their one-block shell; the renderer and the physics mirror now check that shell (`asset::shellDigestOf`) before acting on the key. 14-voxels' p95 frame went from 11.1 to 2.6 ms and its worst from 18 to 6-8 ms. Test: "breaking a block inside a chunk re-meshes that chunk alone (audit P1)". |
| D272 | audit, 2026-09-28 | Every run of 04-obby and 10-open-world warned that "the sound  is not in any mounted content directory" (audit P1) | fixed | A `Sound` that names no content plays the placeholder tone by design, and the examples' sounds are tones; the warning is now for a sound that names a file that is not there. |
| D273 | audit, 2026-09-28 | Characters were walked in a hash map's order to fire `Landed` and to retire them, which differs between MSVC and libstdc++ (audit E6) | fixed | Changed by reading: `PhysicsSync::m_characters` is an ordered map. The island save already sorted its keys; `writeCharacters` and retirement did not. |
| D274 | audit, 2026-09-28 | A rollback kept an anchored part's "kinematic until" tick from after the save (audit E7) | fixed | Changed by reading: `SaveSimulation` carries each body's `movingUntilTick` (format 2) and `RestoreSimulation` puts it back. |
| D275 | audit, 2026-09-28 | Enabling a weld skipped the cycle check, so a loop closed while it was off was accepted (audit E11) | fixed | The new conformance case closes a loop through a disabled weld and enables it; before, the write was accepted. Off to on is now checked as a new pair is. Moving an attachment a weld hangs from is still not checked at the move: the resolver's walk is bounded by the weld count, so such a loop resolves in some order and does not hang. Test: welds.spec, "refuses to enable a weld that would close a cycle". |
| D276 | audit, 2026-09-28 | A streamed world's chunks were torn down in hash order, so instance ids made afterwards differed between compilers (audit E12) | fixed | `StreamingGlue` keeps its chunks in an ordered map. How many chunks materialise in a frame stays a wall-clock budget, by design: when a chunk arrives depends on the disk already, and the budget only decides which frame shows it. |
| D277 | audit, 2026-09-28 | A job run inline -- no slot free, or a payload too large to store -- ran before the jobs it depended on (audit E13) | fixed | Changed by reading: it waits on its dependencies first, helping the pool while it waits. |
| D278 | audit, 2026-09-28 | Whether a part's parent was in the world was remembered for the rest of the tick, even after a script moved the parent out (audit E15) | fixed | Changed by reading: the memo is also keyed by the world's mutation count. |
| D279 | audit, 2026-09-28 | `math.random` was seeded from the clock, so the same world drew different numbers on every run (audit S14) | fixed | It is now seeded from a copy of the world's own stream, which leaves the world's draws as they were. Test: "math.random is the same stream for the same world, run after run": two VMs over worlds with one seed draw the same numbers. |
| D280 | audit, 2026-09-28 | A headless run that joined a match reported "Frame took 5391574 ms" and clamped: the real clock counts from boot, the synthetic one from zero (audit A9) | fixed | `FrameClock::switched` and `FrameScheduler::rebase`: the frame the clock changes measures from the switch. Test: "a clock that becomes another clock is measured from the switch, not from boot". |
| D281 | audit, 2026-09-28 | A minimized window ran its loop flat out on a whole core (audit A13) | fixed | Changed by reading: a windowed frame with no image to present sleeps until the next tick, as a headless one on the real clock already did. |
| D282 | audit, 2026-09-28 | A machine that could not keep up logged the catch-up warning on every clamped frame (audit A14) | fixed | At most once every five seconds. Test: "a machine that cannot keep up says so every few seconds, not every frame": twenty seconds of clamped frames, four warnings. |
| D283 | audit, 2026-09-28 | A shipped game's whole content pack was read into memory when it mounted (audit F10) | fixed | `platform::MappedFile`: a pack opened from a file is mapped, and the system pages in what is read. An APK entry, which is no file, is still read whole. The editor's object store is left as it is: what it hands out is a span callers keep, and dropping an entry under one would be a use after free. Test: "a pack opened from a file is mapped, not read, and answers as one read would". |
| D284 | audit, 2026-09-28 | A block or chunk coordinate near `INT_MAX` overflowed an `i32` in the fill, the mesher and the physics mirror (audit F12) | fixed | Blocks and field chunks reach a million chunks from the origin (`MaxVoxelChunkKey`, `MaxChunkKey`): a write past it is refused, a fill stops at it, and a scene, partition or cell file naming a chunk past it is refused or malformed. Tests: "a block past the world's reach is refused, and a fill up to INT_MAX stops at it", "a chunk key past the field's reach is a malformed file". |
| D285 | audit, 2026-09-28 | A JSON object's keys were found by a walk, quadratic over a large save, and a save file of any size was read whole (audit F13) | fixed | An object of more than sixteen keys is found by binary search, the last of equal keys still winning, and a save file larger than any slot could write reads as a damaged one without being loaded. Tests: "a large object is found by key as a small one is", "a save file larger than any slot could write is not read". |
| D286 | audit, 2026-09-28 | `project.toml` numbers were cast to integers before any range check (audit F14) | fixed | `shadow_resolution = -5` converted to a `u32` with undefined behaviour. Every graphics number and the window size are checked as doubles first, and one out of range is ignored. Test: "numbers no setting can hold are ignored before they are converted". |
| D287 | audit, 2026-09-28 | Save slot names could be Windows devices (`CON`, `NUL`, `COM1`), and two spellings of one name were two slots and one file (audit S15) | fixed | Both refused, the second with its own message (`script.err.save_slot_case`). Tests in `save_tests.cpp`. |
| D288 | audit, 2026-09-28 | The Android tools downloads had no checksum and a cached archive was trusted for ever, in the CLI and in `install-android.ps1`, whose JDK was "the latest 17" (audit T2, T8) | fixed | Each pinned archive's SHA-256 is checked on download and on every reuse: the JDK's and Gradle's as their publishers list them, the command-line tools' computed on 2026-09-29 from the archives whose SHA-1 matched Google's repository. The script pins the JDK's version and downloads beside the file first. |
| D289 | audit, 2026-09-28 | `[project] version` named the archive and the APK unsanitised, and control characters in name, version or company were lines in a `.desktop` file (audit T3, T7) | fixed | The version is held to a file name's characters, and no field keeps a control character. |
| D290 | audit, 2026-09-28 | The dev server relayed any local client's messages to the engine, and relayed the engine's token to every client; the token came from `math.random` (audit T4) | fixed | The token is 32 bytes from the system's random source, the engine's `hello` is relayed without it, and a client's messages reach the engine only after it proves it holds the token. The hot-reload gate checks that the relayed hello carries none. |
| D291 | audit, 2026-09-28 | A remembered keystore password was found by any project that named the keystore's path, and was kept in a file with default permissions (audit T5, T6) | fixed | Remembered for one project and one keystore (`--project`, which the Export window passes), in a file only the user can read on Linux and macOS (under the user's profile on Windows). It is still not encrypted, and the manual says so: a key kept beside it would protect nothing. |
| D292 | audit, 2026-09-28 | `ludwerk saves --player clear` and the engine built the player's folder from an unsanitised company and name (audit T9) | fixed | `platform::pathComponent`, mirrored in the CLI: separators, characters Windows refuses and controls become `-`, and dots and spaces at the ends go. |
| D293 | audit, 2026-09-28 | An APK whose signature did not verify was left in `dist/`, and a gate stage that ran nothing reported `ok` (audit T10) | fixed | The APK is removed when `apksigner` refuses it, and `localgate.ps1` reports a stage that skipped as `skip`. |
| D294 | audit, 2026-09-28 | `[assets] content` could name a folder outside the project, which the build packed into the game (audit T11) | fixed | Refused with `cli.assets.err.content_outside`, by `ludwerk build` and `ludwerk build-assets` alike. |
| D295 | owner, 2026-09-29 | The Smooth brush could not smooth: a held stroke moved a gentle hill a third of a metre and then stopped, and it wore flat ground down a step | fixed | Reproduced (the new tests fail on the old brush: four idle stamps of thirty, 0.23 m, flat ground 255 to 254). It blurred occupancy, which ramps linearly across the surface, so it moved the surface only where the ramp bent, by less than a quantisation step, and each stamp re-read its own rounding. `asset::smoothBall` now rebuilds the signed distance to the surface (exact across the ramp, carried out by a chamfer walk), blurs that with a gaussian half the brush wide, and never rounds a real move to nothing. Flat ground does not move; a hill, a pit, a cliff and bumps smaller than the brush do. Tests: "a held smooth brush keeps wearing a gentle hill down, and never stalls", "smoothing leaves flat ground exactly where it is", "smoothing fills a pit and flattens bumps smaller than the brush". |
| D296 | owner, 2026-09-29 | The terrain brush died whenever Properties came forward in the dock the Terrain panel shares -- which selecting anything, or creating ground, does -- and making ground put the brush down | fixed | The brush now follows the panel being open, not its tab being in front; selecting the terrain or what grows on it no longer puts a terrain brush down; the viewport says when a brush is in hand and that Escape puts it down. The owner's 2026-09-23 wish -- no brush nobody asked for -- is kept by tiles, not tabs, picking a brush up. |
| D297 | audit of the terrain editor, 2026-09-29 | With the Terrain panel open, the Blocks and Tiles tools sculpted the terrain under a click instead of placing a block or a tile | fixed | Only the three terrain tools stroke the terrain. Test: "the block and tile tools never sculpt the terrain under them". |
| D298 | audit of the terrain editor, 2026-09-29 | The foliage brush's Thin set the sculpt brush to Subtract, so the next sculpt stroke dug | fixed | The foliage brush has its own paint-or-thin (`setFoliageThin`). Test: "the foliage brush's paint or thin is its own". |
| D299 | audit of the terrain editor, 2026-09-29 | Creating terrain was two undo steps, and flat ground was laid at a height in the field's space while a heightmap's is the world's | fixed | One step, and a world height. Test: "the first Create Terrain is one undo step, at the world height asked for". |
| D300 | audit of the terrain editor, 2026-09-29 | A brush too big for the voxels did nothing and said nothing | fixed | A refused stroke says why. Test: "a brush too big for the voxels says so instead of doing nothing". |
| D301 | audit of the terrain editor, 2026-09-29 | A stroke that changed nothing still pushed an undo step, and every stamp bumped the terrain's revision whether it changed anything or not | fixed | `UndoStack::retract` takes back a step a stroke did not use; a stamp that changed nothing changes no revision. Test: "a stroke that changes nothing leaves nothing to undo". |
| D302 | audit of the terrain editor, 2026-09-29 | A quick drag with a small brush left a gap: past 64 stamps a frame the stroke was its two ends | fixed | Past the ceiling the stroke is 64 stamps spread along it. Test: "a fast drag with a small brush is a sparser line, never a gap". |
| D303 | audit of the terrain editor, 2026-09-29 | The heightmap form's cost readout assumed half-metre voxels where a new terrain has one-metre ones; the ring was a circle for a square brush and showed nothing of a soft brush's falloff | fixed | The readout uses the default voxel; a square Add or Dig draws its box, and a soft brush a fainter ring where it does half. |
| D304 | the determinism gate, 2026-09-29 | The world hash read a player intent's action by its atom NUMBER, so one method more in a build -- one name interned earlier -- moved the character replay's trace from tick 200 with nothing in the world different | fixed | Hashed by text, as the file's own first rule says. Test: "the world hash reads a player's intents by action name, not by atom number"; the character trace, re-recorded, now replays alike on a build with the method and one without. |
| D305 | the terrain audit, 2026-09-29 | A chunk dug to nothing came back: when its cell streamed out and in again, after an undo or a Stop, and in the file a save wrote -- a digging game on a streamed world lost its holes (G1-G3) | fixed | A load takes no key the package's copy already holds (`TerrainField::shareFrom(from, known)`, and the block world's); a save merges no removed chunk back and removes the file of a cell dug empty after it was let go. Tests: "ground dug to nothing stays dug when its cell goes out and comes back", "a world put back does not bring back a chunk dug before it was taken", "a save does not write back a chunk dug in a cell that was let go". |
| D306 | the terrain audit, 2026-09-29 | A replica lost a removal the server sent before it loaded that cell; the server sent a cell streamed out as a hole when its chunk had been edited back to the package's bytes; and a terrain that appeared after a player was sent the ground never sent its removals (G4, G5) | fixed | The replica keeps the authority's word on a chunk it has not loaded in its `shipped`; a key leaving the ground and the package together is not sent; a new terrain's shadow starts from its package. Tests: "a chunk removed before a replica loaded its cell stays removed when it does", "an edit that came back to the package's bytes is not sent as a hole when the cell streams out". |
| D307 | the terrain audit, 2026-09-29 | An edit where a streamed terrain was not loaded -- a script's, the brush's, a block placed -- made a chunk of only the edit, which shadowed the cell's own for good; Replace with Flat Ground and Clear All Ground acted on the loaded cells only (U1, U2) | fixed | `World::loadGround` reads the cells under an edit first (`FieldStreamer::loadNow`), for every terrain verb, `VoxelService`, the brush, Replace with Flat Ground and heightmap import; Clear All Ground clears the cell index with the field. Test: "an edit where the ground is not loaded yet reads it first". |
| D308 | the terrain audit, 2026-09-29 | `Terrain:RaiseBall` with a huge amount allocated gigabytes a column, and an infinite one was undefined behaviour (B1) | fixed | The amount is held to the band's height and refused when not finite. Conformance: "refuses what no terrain can hold". |
| D309 | the terrain audit, 2026-09-29 | `VoxelService:FillBlocks` had no bound: one call filled a million chunks or walked 10^18 empty ones, and a coordinate past an `i32` was cast unchecked (B2) | fixed | A box of more than 256 blocks cubed is refused (`scene.err.voxel_fill_too_large`); coordinates are clamped before the cast and a NaN refused. Conformance: "refuses a fill no block world could hold". |
| D310 | the terrain audit, 2026-09-29 | `VoxelSize`, `MinHeight` and `MaxHeight` took values a save, a stream and the wire refuse: the ground was lost on reopen and never reached a replica (B3) | fixed | Each setter checks `saneFieldSettings` on the float it becomes. Conformance: "refuses what no terrain can hold". |
| D311 | the terrain audit, 2026-09-29 | An edit far enough out made a chunk past `MaxChunkKey`; the save then refused its whole cell and the wire its whole message (B4) | fixed | `voxelIndex` clamps to the keys a field may hold, and the writer refuses a key out of range. Conformance: "refuses what no terrain can hold". |
| D312 | the terrain audit, 2026-09-29 | `Terrain:SmoothBall` at the largest radius allowed allocated about 1.5 GB (B5) | fixed | Smoothing has its own bound, 160 voxels a side (`MaxSmoothVoxels`), and says so. Conformance: "refuses what no terrain can hold". |
| D313 | the terrain audit, 2026-09-29 | Terrain material ids wrapped through a `u8` (256 erased); a NaN strength turned Smooth and Flatten into a dig; `WriteHeights`' corner and `ReadVoxels`' region were cast unclamped; a brush at a NaN place wrote round the origin (B6) | fixed | Ids are checked 0 to 255, strengths checked finite, corners and regions go through `voxelIndex`, and a box that is not a place is empty. Conformance: "refuses what no terrain can hold". |
| D314 | the terrain audit, 2026-09-29 | A replica dropped the layers and rules sent before it had a terrain, refused every chunk of a terrain whose voxel the server changed while it was empty, and never saw a terrain's `Position` (R1-R3) | fixed | The look makes the terrain too and carries its place (protocol 25); an empty terrain takes the authority's voxel. Tests: "a terrain placed and given its look before any ground reaches a replica as it is", "a scene's empty terrain given another voxel by the server takes it". |
| D315 | the terrain audit, 2026-09-29 | A terrain destroyed and made again by a server script left the old scene's ground on every replica (R4) | fixed | The package the peers loaded stays the base a replacing terrain is measured against, and a destroyed one sends its ground's removal. Test: "a terrain destroyed and made again on the server takes the old ground from every replica". |
| D316 | the terrain audit, 2026-09-29 | A save left `shipped` as the scene was opened, so an edit saved and then undone was never sent; a scene with no block world kept the last one's (R5) | fixed | A save makes what it wrote the package, a restore keeps the keys it had loaded with what the disk now holds, and a scene with no block world clears it. Test: "a restore moves a terrain's revisions on, and its package is what the disk now holds". |
| D317 | the terrain audit, 2026-09-29 | A hostile server's block types were not checked for finite colours, and tens of thousands of fluid reactions were a quadratic stall (R6) | fixed | Non-finite colours drop the message, and more than 4096 reactions are refused. No test: the session tests do not forge a hostile server's bytes. |
| D318 | the terrain audit, 2026-09-29 | A tick with nothing moving retired every terrain collider while the pass still read as settled: a character respawned where it fell stood on nothing (P1) | fixed | A terrain passed over marks its pass unsettled. Test: "a tick with nothing moving does not leave the next one standing on colliders that are gone". |
| D319 | the terrain audit, 2026-09-29 | A small voxel size made every mesh and collider build a stall or an out-of-memory: the sky map's filter walked its whole reach per column (P2) | fixed | A terrain's voxel is at least 0.1 m, the editor's smallest, and the sky's sliding maximum is O(n) (van Herk). Test: "field settings are checked as the floats they are". |
| D320 | the terrain audit, 2026-09-29 | The renderer visited every root in a terrain's bounding box every frame: two edits a million metres apart froze a client (P3) | fixed | Only roots within the view distance are walked, and distance is tested before occupancy. No test of the cost; the loader tests hold what it draws. |
| D321 | the terrain audit, 2026-09-29 | An edit across more chunks than a tick rebuilds rebuilt the same nearest four every tick, and the rest stayed solid where they had been dug (P5) | fixed | Longest waiting first, then nearest. Test: "an edit across more chunks than a tick rebuilds reaches every one of them". |
| D322 | the terrain audit, 2026-09-29 | A restore rolled a terrain's revisions back, so a cached mesh could be trusted over ground the restore had changed; a terrain made again in the same slot filed its meshes under the old one's URN (P6) | fixed | A restore moves every ground revision past any seen; the URN carries the slot's generation. Test: "a restore moves a terrain's revisions on". |
| D323 | the terrain audit, 2026-09-29 | Ctrl+Z during a stroke popped the stroke's own step mid-drag; a stroke that changed nothing cleared the redo stack and took back whatever step was on top (E1, E2) | fixed | Undo and redo wait for a stroke to end, and a stroke records its step at its first change. Tests: "undo waits for a stroke to end", "a stroke that changes nothing leaves what redo would bring back". |
| D324 | the terrain audit, 2026-09-29 | The brush's material was never checked against the terrain's layers: new ground was an id no layer named, and stayed grey after the first material was added (E3) | fixed | Ground is laid as a layer the terrain has, or its first (`Editor::groundMaterial`). Test: "ground is laid as a material the terrain has". |
| D325 | the terrain audit, 2026-09-29 | A click past the ground's edge put an Add on the origin's plane in mid-air; a terrain replaced mid-stroke took the rest of the stroke (E4, E5) | fixed | The plane starts a stroke only on an empty field; a stroke ends with its terrain. Tests: "an Add aimed past the ground puts nothing in mid-air", "a stroke ends with the terrain it began on". |
| D326 | the terrain audit, 2026-09-29 | The terrain keys fired while the game played; the chip and the status bar showed a brush with its panel closed; Paint's hint named keys it has not; Paint armed with no materials; Replace with Flat Ground refused as too large said nothing and left a step (E6) | fixed | Each gated or said: play mode, the panel, Paint's own hint, a status line for Paint with no materials, a refusal message and no step. No test of the panel's drawing. |
| D327 | the terrain audit, 2026-09-29 | A big brush dragged fast could stall a frame for seconds: the stamps a frame were capped and the voxels they walk were not (E7) | fixed | A frame's stamps are bounded by the voxels they walk; the rest of the drag is walked next frame. No test of the time; the stroke tests hold where it stamps. |
| D328 | the owner, 2026-09-29 (a screenshot: Blend at Softness 50%, a hard stepped edge) | A soft paint brush left a hard edge: Softness mixed a hard brush with a soft one, so at 50% the rim still took half the strength, and a stroke's stamps summed there to whole cover a stamp or two behind the middle | fixed | Softness is now how much of the radius fades (the inner rest is the whole strength, the fade ends at nothing on the rim), and a Blend or Erase stamp takes its share of what is left rather than a fixed amount, as an opacity brush does. The ring shows where the brush paints half. `terrain_tests.cpp`: a half-soft brush is whole inside half its radius and nearly nothing at its rim; a stroke of 41 stamps keeps a ramp across its edge. |
| D329 | the owner, 2026-09-29 (the same screenshot) | Two flat-coloured materials painted over each other met in a step at half cover, voxel by voxel, however soft the paint: the height blend's `BlendSharpness` drew an edge where the heights had none to draw | fixed | The blend's width follows the difference in relief at the pixel: none, and it is a crossfade by the cover; relief, and the painted material fills the other's cracks as sharply as its `BlendSharpness` says. A shader: checked by image (flat red over flat green, a stroke at 35% and 50% softness: a hard edge before, a ramp after), with D328's tests for the cover it draws. |
| D330 | the owner, 2026-09-29 ("no terreno não deveria ser possível criar um material, só adicionar os que tenho no Content") | The terrain panel made materials of its own (New Material, Add Starter Materials) and hid the project's behind "Change Layers...", so an imported material looked unusable on a terrain | fixed | As in other engines: a `+` after the swatches picks one of the project's materials (searchable), a material dragged from Content onto the `+` adds it and onto a swatch replaces that layer, and a swatch's right-click replaces, opens or removes it. The panel makes no material; starter terrain materials are Content's *New Terrain Starter Materials*. `editor_tests.cpp`: a terrain is given materials from Content, refuses a duplicate or a non-material, replaces a layer and removes the last. |
| D331 | the script-sides survey, 2026-09-29 (S0.1) | Every package carried `.engine/trash/`: the script files the editor moved aside when their instance went, as source, server code included, in the players' packages too | fixed | `trash` joins the editor-only list of `.engine/` the export leaves out. `tests/packaging/sides.test.luau` puts a sentinel there and finds it in neither side's package. |
| D332 | the script-sides survey, 2026-09-29 (S0.2) | A script could write another script's `Source` and enable it: text the sandbox never loaded, run -- `loadstring` by another name | fixed | A new IDL mark, `ScriptReadOnly` (`PropertyDesc::scriptReadOnly`): the editor, the scene reader and the dev server write the property, and a script's write raises `script.err.property_not_script_writable`. `Source` carries it; ADR 0138's `RunContext` will. `instance/script_source.spec.luau`; the specs that wrote `Source` take their code from stamps (`tests/conformance/content/stamps/`), which the conformance run now finds (D336). |
| D333 | the script-sides survey, 2026-09-29 (S0.3) | A package's scenes, stamps and `global.json` carried their scripts' text: the bytecode option covered `src/` only | fixed | `engine-host --compile-content-scripts` compiles every `Source` in the staged content before it is packed, as `luauc:` and base64 (a JSON string cannot hold bytecode); the scene reader turns it back into bytecode, and the writer writes bytecode that way. Every side is staged now, a one-sided game's too, and a staged file is unlinked before it is written. The sentinel test has a `Workspace` script and a stamp's script; a world-host test boots a compiled scene and its script runs. |
| D334 | the script-sides survey, 2026-09-29 (S0.4), run against the package | A script inside a replicated part ran its file scope once on a joined client and then died with the part: the join cleared the scene after the boot drain | fixed | A replica's boot clears what the authority replicates before the first script starts. Until ADR 0138 §6 such a script does not run on a replica at all. `world_host_tests.cpp`. |
| D335 | the script-sides survey, 2026-09-29 (S0.5, S0.6) | A script that survived a scene change (a kept screen's) was started again beside its running first run, every change; and a script disabled and enabled again had its old run's handlers fire beside the new run's | fixed | The first half of ADR 0137's run record: `ModuleRegistry::runs`, each start's globals table as the run's identity. A running script is never started again; a re-enable ends the old run first; threads, handlers, render steps and message bindings of an ended run are not resumed (`SuppressReason::Ended`). `world_host_tests.cpp` counts a kept script's runs across two changes; `instance/script_enabled.spec.luau` sees one part a heartbeat after a re-enable, not two. |
| D336 | the script-sides work, 2026-09-29 | `Instance.stamp` in a conformance run could not find the suite's own stamps: the stamp source looked under the project's `content/` only | fixed | A conformance run's stamp source also looks under `tests/conformance/content/`, which the run already mounts for materials. |
| D337 | the script-sides survey, 2026-09-29 (S0.7) | A hot reload booted again with the boot's topology and scene: a world that had joined, hosted or changed scene came back solo, in the first scene | fixed | `currentOptions` gives the reload the topology and the scene the world is in now. `reload_tests.cpp`, which fails with the line taken out. |
| D338 | the script-sides survey, 2026-09-29 (S0.8) | `ludwerk check`'s header announced an i18n lint `run` never called | fixed | Corrected rather than called: the lint checks the engine's own `ENG_TR` keys, which a game has none of, so it is the repository's (`tools/repo/i18nlint.luau`), run by the gate. The header and `api-design.md` say so. |
| D339 | the script-sides survey, 2026-09-29 (ADR 0137 §1) | A script that came into the world at run time never started: a clone parented, a model carrying scripts parented, a stamp placed with `Instance.stamp`. Only boot, a scene load and an `Enabled` write started scripts | fixed | `World::setParent` queues the scripts of every subtree it moves; the runtime's drain starts each that is now live and not running (ADR 0137's amendment). `instance/script_live.spec.luau`. |
| D340 | the script-sides survey, 2026-09-29 (ADR 0137 §1) | A running script that left the world went on running: parented to nil, moved into storage, or carried out by the model holding it | fixed | The same queue: a queued script that is running and no longer live stops, and its threads and handlers are suppressed. Coming back is a fresh run. `instance/script_live.spec.luau`. |
| D341 | the script-sides survey, 2026-09-29 (ADR 0137 §3), run against the package | A `Script` in `ServerStorage` or `ReplicatedStorage` ran -- a template running itself, and on a joined client too | fixed | Storage is inert: `scriptLive` refuses a script with either above it, and a scene that has one logs `scene.warn.script_in_storage` once per storage when it loads. **Breaking** for a game that relied on it. `instance/script_live.spec.luau`. |
| D342 | the script-sides survey, 2026-09-29 (ADR 0137 §5) | A machine back in solo after a join ran only its file-mounted server code: the scene's server scripts and the parts the join had replaced were gone. A Join or a Host checked no script against its new side | fixed | `WorldHost::returnToSolo` loads the current scene again, restarts the server code and checks every script; a Join and a Host check every script too (`reconcileAllScripts`). `network_session_tests.cpp`, "back solo after a join". |
| D343 | the script-sides survey, 2026-09-29 (ADR 0137 §6) | A `SubWorld` on a dedicated server ran its client scripts: a sub-world always booted solo | fixed | A sub-world boots dedicated when the world holding it is. `sub_world_tests.cpp`, "a sub-world on a dedicated server". |
| D344 | the script-sides audit, 2026-09-29 (P1) | A hostile server could grow a client's name table without end, and make it read a file per spawn: each spawn's `stamp:` origin was interned before anything asked whether the client's package held that stamp | fixed | A stamp origin is interned only when the package holds the stamp; a name it does not hold is remembered, up to 64, and past that none is read (`ScriptTemplates::stampAsset`). `network_session_tests.cpp`: 200 made-up names grow nothing; 150 distinct ones cost at most 64 reads. |
| D345 | the script-sides audit, 2026-09-29 (P1) | A game that is not dedicated (players host, or one plays alone) shipped without `global.json`'s `Client` folder: its staging stripped `global.json` as if it were the server's side. From S0.3 | fixed | `stripGlobal` keeps both folders for such a package. `export.test.luau`; `sides.test.luau` now builds `host` and `none` too and finds every sentinel of both sides. |
| D346 | the script-sides audit, 2026-09-29 (P1) | A script moved in the tick a scene changed was never answered: the scene's start walk dropped the moved-scripts queue, so one taken out of the world went on running and one put into `GlobalScriptService` never started | fixed | The start walks answer the queue: a moved script running and no longer live stops, and one under the kept service that became live starts. `world_host_tests.cpp`, "a script moved in the tick a scene changes". |
| D347 | the script-sides audit, 2026-09-29 (P1) | Every scene change kept every script it closed: a run's record was overwritten when its slot was reused, without letting go of the old globals table | fixed | The record lets its old table go, and the scene's destroyed scripts' runs end (D346). `world_host_tests.cpp`: eight changes of a scene holding 6 MB held 100 MB; now under 16. |
| D348 | the script-sides audit, 2026-09-29 (P1) | A stopped script came back from a wait: `WaitForChild`, a remote answer, an HTTP request, a save, a preload resumed its thread with no check of its run | fixed | `resumeScheduled` drops a thread whose run is over, as a timer's or a signal's already was. `script_live.spec.luau`, "does not go on past a wait". |
| D349 | the script-sides audit, 2026-09-29 (P1) | A linked stamp's overrides escaped the package strip: an override that made a stamp's script server code, with its text edited, shipped that text to players; one that made it client code left players with none | fixed | The strip resolves a linked instance against the stamp's own file: the other side's code becomes an empty `Source` override, and this side's, when the stamp file lost it, is carried in the override. `export.test.luau`. |
| D350 | the script-sides audit, 2026-09-29 (P1) | `.engine/conflicts/` (the editor's copy of a script file that changed under it, server code included) and `.engine/saves/` (the developer's save slots) shipped in every package | fixed | Both are editor-only. `export.test.luau`; `sides.test.luau` puts a sentinel in each and finds it in no package. |
| D351 | the script-sides audit, 2026-09-29 (P1) | Edited in the editor, then played and hosted, a scene's instances were numbered as they were read, not as the saved file a client reads: every instance after an inserted one attached the wrong scripts on a client | fixed | Play renumbers every carried tree as a read of the saved file would (`scene::renumberOrigins`). `world_host_tests.cpp`. |
| D352 | the script-sides audit, 2026-09-29 (P2) | An origin named a scene's tree and not the scene: an instance that outlived its scene took the next scene's scripts on a replica | fixed | An origin is `scene:<path>#<tree>`; a load names its scene before it reads; a replica drops the scene before's templates on a change. `world_host_tests.cpp`. |
| D353 | the script-sides audit, 2026-09-29 (P2) | The package strip read `RunContext` inside the script services too, where the service decides: a server-marked script in `ClientScriptService` lost its code in the client's package | fixed | The strip leaves the deciding services' and `global.json`'s `Server`/`Client` folders' scripts to the service. `export.test.luau`. |
| D354 | the script-sides audit, 2026-09-29 (P2) | Every world queued the scripts it moved, and only a script runtime drains the queue: the editor's stages, the preview renderer and the partitioner grew theirs for ever | fixed | A world queues only once a script runtime says it drains it (`World::setTracksScripts`). `world_host_tests.cpp`. |
| D355 | the script-sides audit, 2026-09-29 (P2) | Any saved string that began like bytecode, an attribute's included, was written as `luauc:` and read back as that literal; and a package compiled an attribute called `Source` as a script, failing the build or corrupting it | fixed | Only a code property is written as bytecode, and the package compiler finds `Source` only in `properties` and in override entries, by the JSON's structure. `world_host_tests.cpp`. |
| D356 | the script-sides audit, 2026-09-29 (P2) | A script that cleared its own `script` global made its threads and handlers unstoppable: a run was known by that global | fixed | A run is known by its globals table, kept in a weak registry table the script cannot reach. `script_live.spec.luau`, "stops even when it cleared its own script global". |
| D357 | the script-sides audit, 2026-09-29 (P2) | In the editor, with nothing playing, writing `Enabled` true on a script started it | fixed | A re-enable starts nothing before the world's scripts have (ADR 0058). `world_host_tests.cpp`. |
| D358 | the script-sides audit, 2026-09-29 (P2) | A hot reload that could not find the current scene loaded the boot scene and named the current one | fixed | The file, the text and the path change together or not at all. `reload_tests.cpp`. |
| D359 | the script-sides audit, 2026-09-29 (P2) | The Explorer's mark for a script for both sides did not show: green on a green script icon | fixed | Tinted `neutral`. Seen in the editor, driven by real input. |
| D360 | ludwerk-08, 2026-09-29, by running (P1) | A stopped script's connections stayed on their signals for ever, suppressed: cloning and destroying fifty scripted projectiles a tick, each connected to `Heartbeat`, made a tick cost 19 ms after 137 500 of them, growing without bound | fixed | A connection knows its run; a run's end disconnects them all, drops its timers and releases its globals table after the drain (`finishEndedRuns`). `world_host_tests.cpp`, "a script that stops takes its connections and its waits with it": 2 181 connections left, now as many as are alive. |
| D361 | text input T6, 2026-09-30, by running a game with real input (P1) | A paste right after a copy pasted nothing: a clipboard history and a launcher open the clipboard about 200 ms after every change, SDL gives up opening it after 30 ms, and its read answered an empty string as though the clipboard held one | fixed | A read that finds text on the clipboard and gets none, or a write that is refused, is tried again every 10 ms for up to a quarter of a second (`clipboardTextFrom`). `platform_tests.cpp`, "text on the clipboard that another program holds open for a moment is still read". |
| D362 | text input T6, 2026-09-30, by running a game with real input (P1) | A click whose press and release landed between two frames was lost -- a tap, a quick click, a touch -- and a quick double click lost its second click: the host read a click from the button's state at each frame, not from its events | fixed | The host reads the frame's presses and releases as events, and says when the release came first; the interaction then ends the first click before it starts the second. `interaction_tests.cpp`, "a click inside one frame, and a double click whose middle is one frame, activate every time": one activation, now two. |
| D363 | terrain audit TA5, 2026-09-29, by reading (P0) | `ReadVoxels` and `WriteVoxels` could be made to loop past any bound: each side of a region can be 2^27 voxels, three of them multiplied as integers passed 2^64 and wrapped to a count the limit let through, and the loops then ran the true sizes; and `WriteVoxels` cast a NaN or huge size straight to an integer | fixed | The region's count saturates, computed in doubles; a size that is not a finite count from 0 to the largest region, or an occupancy that is not a finite number, is refused by a keyed error before a voxel is written. `terrain.spec.luau`, "refuses a region too large to count, and a size or an occupancy that is not a number (TA5)". |
| D364 | terrain audit TA15, 2026-09-29, by reading (P1) | Two edits had no memory bound: `WriteHeights` never refused, so a steep table asked for hundreds of thousands of dense chunks, and `GrowBall` (the editor's Raise and Lower) copied a box the general edit limit allowed -- a quarter of a gigabyte for one stamp | fixed | `WriteHeights` estimates what it would lay from its table first and refuses past `MaxHeightVoxels`; `GrowBall` is held to smoothing's bound, as it copies its box the same way. `terrain_tests.cpp`, "Raise and Lower on a ball too large to hold, and heights too steep to lay, are refused whole (TA15)". |
| D365 | terrain audit TA4, 2026-09-29, by running (P0) | Smooth dug holes: `SmoothBall(center, 8, 1)` on ground 4 m thick cut through it, and five passes over a ball on flat ground took its top from 7.94 m to below the ground it stood on, the ring round it sinking each pass. The blur of a distance mixed a thin slab's top with its bottom, and the blur of that V is air | fixed | Each surface is smoothed on its own, along the axis it faces: a crossing moves towards the same surface round it, in flux form so the ground's volume is kept, and never past the next surface in its row. `terrain_tests.cpp`, "smoothing a thin slab opens no hole in it..." and "smoothing a ball on flat ground again and again never digs below the ground, and settles". |
| D366 | terrain audit TA16, 2026-09-29, by reading (P1) | A streamed terrain lost ground six ways: an edit loaded at most 256 of the cells it reached and wrote only itself over the rest; `VoxelSize` changed while cells were on disk, which then came in at the wrong scale; `Terrain:Clear()` kept the cells on disk, which streamed back; a moved terrain kept its cells' bounds where it had stood; `ReadVoxels`, `HeightAt`, `ApplyRules` and the raycast read only loaded cells, so a copy over ground not loaded wrote air; and a cell could be saved past `MaxCellChunks`, then never load | fixed | A load is all or nothing -- up to 4 096 cells, or refused before anything changes, by a keyed error the editor shows too; the read verbs and the raycast load first; `VoxelSize` is refused while an index names cells, and a cell of other settings is refused on arrival; `Clear` drops the index; the streamer moves its terrain cells' bounds with the terrain; a cell past the limit is not written and the save says so. `field_streamer_tests.cpp` (TA16a, b, d) and `terrain_ground_tests.cpp` (TA16a, c, e). |
| D367 | terrain audit TA17c, 2026-09-29, by reading (P1) | The editor's three actions over a whole terrain -- Replace Material everywhere, Apply Rules to voxels, Export Heightmap -- touched only the loaded cells; the export had black holes where cells were not | fixed | Each loads all of the terrain's ground (or the exported square) first, or refuses with the same words as a script. |
| D368 | terrain audit TA8, 2026-09-29, by reading; measured 2026-09-30 (P1) | Terrain shadowed itself at a low sun: the shadow pass drew it with no culling and pushed it from the light by six texels whatever its slope, and a receiver's filter reads up to seven texels off -- on ground the light meets at a slant those are deeper by the slope times the reach. On rounded hills at 8 degrees, 180 000 of a picture's 500 000 pixels facing the sun were darkened, 355 000 at `ShadowSoftness` 1, and every ball's terminator was faceted with it | fixed | `terrain_shadow.hlsl` culls back faces and pushes each fragment by its own depth slope times the cascade's filter reach, plus a texel, up to where the light meets the ground at a tenth (ADR 0142); the receiver's normal offset takes the mesh's normal, not the one a layer bends. `terrain_shadow_acne`: 66 pixels at most, on the smallest ball's terminator. |
| D369 | terrain audit TA9, 2026-09-29, by reading; measured 2026-09-30 (P1) | Contact shadows stippled ground at a slant: the depth buffer is read at pixel centres and a marched point lands anywhere in a pixel, so on ground forty metres off the depth read was tens of centimetres from the depth under the point, against a bias of a few, and at a low sun the ray runs along the ground. 5 600 pixels of a plain at 12 degrees, and a speckle along every sphere's rim towards the sun | fixed | The ray carries the plane it starts on -- one over depth, affine across the screen -- to each pixel it reads, and a pixel on that plane is not a hit. A bias grown by the slant was tried first and lost the contact of balls standing on such ground. `terrain_shadow_acne`: 16 pixels at most; the contact and instanced goldens re-recorded without the rim speckle. |
| D370 | terrain audit TA10, 2026-09-29, by reading (P2) | The far cascade pushed the terrain six of its texels whatever the slope -- about two metres -- so a shadow the ground cast detached from it, and the blend band mixed two such pushes | fixed | A texel plus the slope times the filter's reach, which in the far cascade is its floor of a texel and a half (D368's fix); face-on ground is pushed a texel. `shadow_tests.cpp`, "the terrain's push in a cascade...". |
| D371 | terrain audit P2, 2026-09-29, by reading (P2) | A local light's shadow did not push the terrain at all, so a lamp's tile held exactly the depth the ground was then compared against | fixed | The local tile pushes by the slope times its filter's reach, as a cascade does (ADR 0142). Photographed: a lamp over plain ground, a ball and a floating part, no acne. |
| D372 | terrain audit P2 and photos/08, 2026-09-29, by running (P2) | Bright streaks at a low sun and dark notches under a lamp, a few metres long, on flat ground with no material, at full detail too: the grain's value noise read each lattice point through a float hash so sensitive to its last bit that one point hashed to two values from two cells, and the noise stepped along that cell's edge; the grain's normal is the noise's slope | fixed | An integer hash of the lattice point's coordinates (`terrainLattice`). `--debug-view=bend` draws what the shading does to the mesh's normal, and `terrain_shadow_acne` counts steps in it with `imgsteps`: 73 to 454 a picture before, none after. |
| D373 | terrain audit T3, 2026-09-30, by running (P1) | Since the terrain was built off the main thread (4a7405f3), a headless run with `--screenshot` and no `--screenshot-every` photographed no terrain: thirty frames run flat out are over before a worker has built any | fixed | Any run that takes a picture builds its terrain in the frame, as a run photographing on a schedule already did. |
| D374 | terrain audit TA12, 2026-09-29, by running (P1) | A terrain with no materials was not the plain matte grey ADR 0113's amendment promised: the ground's variation tinted it warm and cool by about six per cent, brown blotches, ball-sized on a ball | fixed | The variation is taken off in proportion to how much of a pixel is plain ground, through paint and rules. `--debug-view=albedo`; `terrain_shadow_acne` holds plain ground to one grey: its colours spread 2 to 10 levels before, none after. |
| D375 | terrain audit TA13, 2026-09-29, by reading (P1) | A terrain layer read its metallic-roughness map's R as occlusion and as height; glTF leaves R undefined, and an ordinary map (R 0) drew its ground's ambient at four tenths and lost every height blend. The material's height map was never read | fixed | The layer arrays are drawn from the maps (`terrain_pack.hlsl`), the surface array packed with the height map's height in R; occlusion is the height where there is a height map and none where there is not. `terrain_material_channels`: an empty-R map and no map draw the same (518 400 pixels differed, by up to 32 levels, on the old reading). |
| D376 | terrain audit P2, 2026-09-29, by reading (P2) | A layer's normal map bent the ground along the image's V, which runs down the image, where glTF's +Y is up it: every bump lit from the wrong side along one axis, on every plane | fixed | Each plane bends against its second axis. `terrain_material_channels`: a map tilted up the image leans flat ground to -Z (blue 179 on the old sign, none now). |
| D377 | terrain audit P2, 2026-09-29, by reading (P2) | The grain's normal nudge went through a frame whose tangent was always +X -- not at right angles to the ground, and degenerate on a wall facing X -- with each plane's slope laid on the same two directions | fixed | The grain's bend is built in world space, each plane's slope along its own axes; `terrain_shadow_acne`'s bend run holds it free of steps. |
| D378 | terrain audit P2, 2026-09-29, by reading (P2) | A terrain's layer arrays were copied from mip 0 of each map with one bilinear tap per texel, so maps over 1 024 texels shimmered; the stand-in arrays were made without contents; and `normalize(input.Normal)` had no guard, so a degenerate triangle's NaN could spread through bloom | fixed | The layers are drawn from the mip that fits; the stand-ins are cleared when made; the terrain's normals fall back instead of dividing by nothing. By construction -- no picture showed the NaN. |
| D379 | terrain audit T3, 2026-09-30, by running (P0) | On Vulkan -- Linux and Android -- no terrain layer ever drew its textures, and the terrain's own params read as nothing: SDL_GPU binds a uniform block to Vulkan with a range of 4 KiB (`MAX_UBO_SECTION_SIZE`), and the terrain's block was 13 600 bytes, its layers' rows first. Found when `terrain_material_channels` passed on Windows and read an unbent normal on the Linux gate | fixed | The layers' rows are a storage buffer per terrain (`GpuTerrainLayer`, `t16 space2`); the block keeps the rules and params, 1 312 bytes; `kMaxUniformBlockBytes` holds every block the renderer sizes to 4 KiB at compile time. `terrain_material_channels` passes on the Linux gate. The block world's palette, 16 400 bytes, is the same defect and the next fix. |
| D380 | terrain audit T3, 2026-09-30, by reading after D379; seen by running (P0) | On Vulkan -- Linux and Android -- every block's sides and underside drew black, with none of its images: the block world's palette was a vertex-stage uniform block of 16 400 bytes, SDL_GPU binds one to Vulkan 4 KiB at a time, and only the blocks' tops were in the first 4 KiB. A pile of red blocks on the Linux gate: tops red, sides (3, 11, 21) | fixed | The registry is a storage buffer (`GpuVoxelBlock`, `t0 space0`), put up when it changes; the block keeps the block size and the atlas's layout, 16 bytes. `block_faces_colours` probes a top and two sides on every backend. |
| D381 | terrain audit P2, 2026-09-29, by reading (P2) | What the game was told the ground is -- a raycast's `Material`, what `ApplyRules` wrote -- ignored paint: ground painted sand and drawn as sand was grass to the game, and ADR 0113 section 2's promised agreement test did not exist | fixed | `drawnMaterial` takes the voxel: its paint from half its cover, then any rule by the voxel's own layer, as the shader weighs them; a raycast carries the paint; `ApplyRules` writes only what a rule draws and leaves paint no rule covers. `terrain_rules_tests.cpp` (three cases); and `terrain_rules_agree` draws the ground by its rules and from what `ApplyRules` wrote, `--debug-view=material`, agreeing but for edges -- a slope the CPU read a fifth too shallow fails it. |
| D382 | terrain audit P2, 2026-09-29, by reading (P2) | Foliage shadows ignored the card's alpha and the instance's fade: a card cast a solid rectangle, and an instance fading at its draw distance cast its whole shadow | fixed | `foliage_shadow.hlsl` reads the UV and the fade, dithers the fade as the forward pass does, and cuts at the material's cutoff by its image's alpha. `foliage_card_shadows`: a card half cut away casts 0.47 of the whole card's shadow; without the cut it cast more than the whole one. |
| D383 | ludwerk-08, 2026-09-30, by running the owner's place (P0) | Pale plates of ground floating past the far edge, a different few each run, and loaded ground not drawn round them: a terrain node whose first mesh was of nothing -- ground dug to air leaves its chunks, and a streamed cell can come in before the one with the surface -- was drawn by nothing, and a load was asked for only of a node never built. When its ground came the node kept its empty mesh, its children were not shown while it stood, and the ground under it was never drawn; what was drawn round it floated. Which nodes it caught depended on what had streamed in when | fixed | A built node with nothing but empty meshes, all of other ground than the field's now, is asked for again like a load. `terrain_loader_tests.cpp`, "a node built empty is built again when ground comes back under it" (32 of 38 checks failed before), and "ground a streamer let go is not drawn from the meshes it had"; on the owner's place five runs draw the same ground, and the plates are gone. |
| D384 | the terrain audit, TA17a (P1) | Add made ground in the air: past the ground's edge a running stroke aims at the level plane it began on, and a held Add stamped a ball there that grew, and a drag walked a bridge of them into the sky -- the owner's floating ball | fixed | An Add aimed at the plane stamps nothing on a field that has ground; the stroke runs on and stamps again when the pointer is back on ground. An empty field has only the plane, and a first Add starts there. `editor_tests.cpp`, "an Add carried off the ground puts no ground in the air (TA17a)". |
