| D385 | the terrain audit, TA17b (P1) | A click with a terrain brush on a part sculpted the ground behind the part: the brush drives before the pick and casts at the terrain alone | fixed | At a stroke's start, a part nearer than the ground along the ray is the click's, and the pick takes it. `editor_tests.cpp`, "a click with the brush on a part selects the part and sculpts nothing behind it (TA17b)". |
| D386 | the terrain audit's editor list (P2) | Apply to Voxels and Replace a material everywhere edited the whole field's box at once, past what one edit may touch on any large terrain (the owner's place is four times it): both were refused whole, and the rules said "the voxels already are what the rules draw". A pass that changed nothing was backed out with an undo, which spent whatever was there to redo | fixed | Both walk the field a chunk at a time -- they decide voxel by voxel, only where there is ground -- in one undo step, recorded only once something changed; a refusal says so. `editor_tests.cpp`, "replacing a material and applying rules reach a terrain too big for one edit" and "applying rules writes what they draw in one step, and a pass that changes nothing keeps the redo". |
| D387 | the terrain audit's editor list (P2) | Hills and a heightmap import on a world with no terrain were two undo steps (the terrain, then the ground), and both answered success when `writeHeights` refused the heights as too steep, with nothing laid | fixed | The terrain's making is the one step; a refusal takes back the terrain it made and says why. `editor_tests.cpp`, "hills on a world with no terrain are one step, and a refusal leaves no terrain and no step". |
| D388 | the terrain audit's editor list (P2) | An exported heightmap did not import as the same ground: the export writes black at the world's floor and white at its ceiling, and the import's fields stood at 0 and 64 m. A second export wrote over the first without a word | fixed | An export leaves `<name>.heightmap.json` beside the image with its size and heights, and choosing that image fills the import's fields; an export takes the next free name. `editor_tests.cpp`, "an exported heightmap imports as the same ground, and a second export keeps the first". |
| D389 | the terrain audit's editor list (P2) | A rule whose lowest was past its highest drew nothing, silently; Add Rule gave a new height rule layer 4, whatever layer 4 was | fixed | A rule's bounds are kept in order, in the panel and by `setTerrainRules`; Add Rule takes the layer named snow, or the last. `editor_tests.cpp`, "a rule's bounds are kept in order". |
| D390 | the terrain audit's data list (P2) | A Terrain edit or read with a NaN or infinite place, or a NaN, infinite or negative radius, changed nothing and returned 0, which a script cannot tell from ground that already was what it asked for; `HeightAt` was typed `number` and returns nil where there is no ground | fixed | Each is an argument error; `HeightAt` is `number?`. `tests/conformance/world/terrain.spec.luau`, "refuses a place or a reach that is not a finite number, rather than answering zero". |
| D391 | the terrain audit's editor list (P2) | The brush ring stayed where the pointer left the viewport; the brush chip showed with no terrain in the world, and was cut at the right edge of a narrow viewport; a fixed Flatten height started at 0 m wherever the ground was; Foliage's Grow planted nothing where nothing was thinned, and said nothing | fixed | Off the image the brush aims at nothing unless a stroke is under way; the chip needs a terrain and shortens to fit; the fixed height starts at the ground the brush last touched; Grow is Restore, and a stroke with nothing to restore says where a layer grows is decided. `editor_tests.cpp`, "the brush aims at nothing with the pointer off the viewport, unless a stroke is under way". |
| D392 | the terrain audit, TA18 (P1) | Any terrain edit rebuilt every navmesh tile the terrain reached: the terrain's revision was in every tile's fingerprint, so one crater rebuilt the navmesh of the world (49 of 49 tiles on a 192 m field) | fixed | A tile's fingerprint carries the chunks of the columns it meshes, whole, and the face or edge of the columns round them its mesher reads (`TerrainChunk::borderDigest`). `navigation_tests.cpp`, "a dig rebuilds the navmesh tiles over it and no others (terrain audit TA18)": 4 or fewer now, 49 before. |
| D393 | the terrain audit, T5 (P4) | Digging beside a character cost 10.3 ms a tick, worst 38.7: the chunks round it had their colliders remeshed one after another on the main thread, each paying for the sky term and the geomorph only drawing reads; and a terrain moved by its `Position` remeshed every collider it had | fixed | A tick's rebuilds meshed side by side on the pool and committed in the world's order; `MeshRegion::collider` meshes the triangles alone; a moved terrain's bodies are moved. `tests/bench/terrain_dig` (2.0 ms, worst 3.8); `physics_sync_tests.cpp`, "the colliders a tick rebuilds are the same built on the pool as one after another"; `terrain_mesher_tests.cpp`, "a collider's mesh is the drawn mesh's points and triangles, without the sky". |
| D394 | the terrain audit, T5; the owner's ruling of 2026-09-30 (P1) | The ground was not one surface where two chunks' colliders met: each chunk's border edge was an edge of its own mesh, and a body crossing met its normal -- a frictionless ball hopped 12 cm, a character at 8 m/s lost a third of a metre a second for a tick and rose 1.6 cm, a car on cylinder-cast wheels felt a 153 N break in its suspension where one surface gives 53. And a ball rolling with friction hopped 4.5 cm on any triangulated ground, seam or none: nothing removed the edges inside one mesh | fixed | ADR 0143: each chunk's collider carries a band of its neighbours' triangles that only lends its edges, refused in contacts; dynamic bodies and characters remove internal edges. Chosen over a compound, a ring and a contact listener by `engine/scene/tests/terrain_seam_tests.cpp`, which drives balls, a character, a resting box and Jolt's `VehicleConstraint` across seams against one mesh with no seam, and fails in 33 places with the old join. |
| D395 | the terrain audit, closing it with the repeat broken up (P2) | A script could not read or write a material's terrain properties -- `TileSize`, `HeightMap`, `Triplanar`, `BlendSharpness` -- though the API has named them since ADR 0113: `copy.TileSize` raised "Material has no member named TileSize". The binding registered the fields a part reads and stopped there | fixed | Every material field is bound, a `static_assert` on the field count keeps a new one from being missed, and `world/material.spec.luau` reads and writes each. |
| D396 | the owner, painting sand on grass in the editor, 2026-09-30 (P1) | Painted ground showed the ground under the paint as a line inside the painted patch. Paint blended until it is all that shows becomes the ground, with nothing over it; beside paint not yet there, a vertex averaged it as a cover of nothing -- over a level-0 cell's corners and over a coarse cell's vertices alike -- and a triangle interpolated from it to a painted corner, so the cover fell to half along the line and the grass showed in the sand's cracks | fixed | A corner, a vertex or a cell whose own ground is the paint painted round it counts as covered wholly by it, in the mesher at every level and in the shader across a triangle. `terrain_levels_tests.cpp` checks the line at levels 0 and 1, and `terrain_paint_edge` photographs it: 92 levels of spread before, 33 after. |
| D397 | the terrain audit, T5; ludwerk-08's pictures of the owner's place at 1 000 m | Ground streamed by its distance through the air: a camera 1 200 m up with the 1 024 m radius had no ground under it at all, and coming down it saw the loaded cells' corners as a sawtooth edge. | fixed | A cell of ground (terrain and voxels) is measured across the ground, the focus taken to the cell's own height (`StreamingLayerRadii::planar`): the ground under a camera is the ground under a person standing there. `streaming_tests.cpp` puts a focus 1 200 m over the grid. |
| D398 | ludwerk-08, the owner's place from a kilometre after T5 (P1) | A streamed terrain wider than its load radius ended where its loaded cells did, in the cells' 64 m corners: a sawtooth on the horizon from any camera that could see past the radius, and from far enough no ground at all. | fixed | ADR 0144: the loader draws the whole terrain, building a node whose ground is not resident from its cells on disk by the same function, a row of cells at a time. `terrain_far` holds the streamed picture to the whole ground's, pixel for pixel. |
| D399 | the owner, from a friend trying the editor, 2026-10-01 (P1) | A `Water` could not be moved in the editor: not clicked in the viewport, no gizmo on it when selected from the Explorer. The same held for every class placed in the world without being a `PVInstance` -- `WaterPoint`, `Terrain`, `Decal`, `NavigationLink`, `Tilemap2D` -- each movable only by typing its numbers. | fixed | The gizmo locates and moves each by how it is placed (a water on its surface, a river by its points, a decal relative to its part, a link by both ends); a water is clicked on its surface and a terrain on its ground, and the rest show a marker. The scale gizmo writes `Size` only where there is one. `editor_tests` sweeps the class registry: a class with a writable `Position`, `CFrame` or `From` that gets no gizmo fails by name. |
| D400 | the owner, "muito lag durante a edição do terreno", measured by ludwerk-08, 2026-10-01 (P0) | Smoothing terrain in the editor froze it: p95 420 ms and p99 561 ms a frame on the owner's place, 210 frames over 33 ms in 30 drags, worse stroke after stroke; one `SmoothBall` at radius 8 cost 55 to 83 ms. | fixed | A stamp read a margin four times its brush and found crossings along every row of it through a clamped lookup per voxel; it reads the margin a crossing needs and the rows within the kernel, a byte at a time, and runs its rows on the job pool with the same result (R10). Every brush reads through the chunk it read last. Radius 8: 2.3 ms; the editor drive p95 14.4 ms, none over 33 ms. `tests/bench/terrain_smooth` and `terrain_paint` hold it in `perf_budget`, and `terrain_tests` pins the ground every brush leaves. |
| D401 | the owner's queue of 2026-09-27, Q0; met again running the gate's tests side by side, 2026-10-01 (P2) | Two runs of the engine from one folder shared one `engine.log`: the second could not open it and logged to the console alone with a warning -- or, where the file could be renamed, rotated it out from under the run still writing it. A match tested from one folder always did this, and eighteen example tests failed for the warning when run at once. | fixed | `core::openLogFileBeside`: a log another process holds is left to it and never rotated; the second run writes `engine_2.log`, up to `engine_9.log`. On POSIX an advisory lock gives the one-writer rule Windows' share mode already gave. `log_tests` holds it. |
| D402 | the owner, sculpting in the editor: "uma malha se cruzando formando um X ... passo um smooth e resolve", 2026-10-01 (P1) | Where two sheets of the terrain's surface passed through one lattice cell -- a wall a voxel thin, two hollows a corner apart -- the mesher joined them at the cell's one vertex: four faces on one edge, an X in section, and triangles lit from behind beside it. A fuzz of random sculpts found up to 27 such edges in 16 000 triangles. | fixed | A cell has a vertex for each sheet that passes through it (`cellSheets`), and a quad takes each cell's vertex for the sheet its edge is on; a vertex whose gradient points against its own faces takes their normal. `terrain_mesh_validation_tests` sculpts 16 seeds of 24 random brushes and holds the surface closed, wound one way and uncrossed; what is left is a neck thinner than a voxel, under one triangle in two thousand. Meshes with one sheet a cell are unchanged. |

The same camera with `FarPlane = 20000` shows the world whole, its mountain where the height function puts it; 278 000 triangles, median frame 2.2 ms. Out to the far plane's corners, so a node goes only when wholly past the plane and the ground ends on a line: `terrain_far_plane` photographs it, and `terrain_loader_tests` holds ground nine kilometres off drawn by a camera that sees twenty and not by one that sees five. |
| D404 | found the same day it was made, 2026-10-01: tests crashing after a virtual was added to `rhi::IDevice` (P0) | With sccache in front of a localised MSVC (ADR 0148), the build recorded no header dependencies: `ninja -t deps` showed 952 of 1 260 objects with none. sccache compiles in a server with no console, where `cl` writes its `/showIncludes` note ("Observação: incluindo arquivo:") in another codepage than the prefix CMake read, so ninja matched nothing -- and every build after a header edit reused objects compiled against the old header. D040's failure by another road, live on the Windows lane from 4cff3d3d until it was found. | fixed | The cache is not used where the compiler's note is not ASCII (`CMakeLists.txt`); the tree was rebuilt clean; and the Windows gate asks an object for its headers after every build and fails on none (`localgate.ps1`), which holds D040 as well. | D405 | found building the session cache (ADR 0149), 2026-10-01, by measuring an import's memory (P1) | A streamed cell whose read came back after the focus had left was kept for ever: the manager held its bytes, decoded and unwanted, until the cell was wanted again -- which for a camera that never returns is never. Parts and ground alike. | fixed | `StreamingManager::tick` lets go of a chunk that is decoded and not wanted -- it was never materialised, so nothing is evicted -- and it is read again if somebody comes back. `streaming_tests.cpp`, "a chunk that arrives after the focus has left is let go, not kept (D405)": 9 held before, none now. |
| D406 | found building the session cache (ADR 0149), 2026-10-01, by reading (P1) | The far ground went on drawing a saved cell as its file was before the save: what a streamed terrain's far nodes read of a cell that is not resident is a summary read once and kept (ADR 0144), and a save that wrote the cell left the summary standing. Dig, save, walk away, and the horizon showed the ground undug until the editor was reopened. | fixed | The cell source is told of each cell a save writes or empties, and of each written to the session cache (`TerrainCellSource::update`), and reads those again; every other cell keeps its summary. `field_streamer_tests.cpp`, "the far ground reads a saved cell again, and keeps what it read of every other (D406)". |

The image is brought to the runner's standard library with the next change to the Tier-2 image (`scripts/docker/tier2.Dockerfile`), so that the local stage refuses what CI refuses; until it is, a file that uses an algorithm names `<algorithm>`. CI's Linux build is the test: red on `b87fe770`, green on `c1ad0e8b` -- whose message gives this the number after: it was taken out of order, the documentation gate was not run before that push, and it said so in CI. | D408 | found flying over the sixteen-kilometre world (ADR 0150), 2026-10-01, by measuring what the streaming held (P1) | A streamed cell's bytes were kept after the cell was made of them. `entry.raw = {}` assigns an empty vector, which keeps the capacity the old one had; every entry the manager had ever loaded went on holding its file's length for as long as the index stood. | D417 | the owner, 2026-10-01: logs launched by ground edited under them in Play fell through the ground they landed on -- "the fast ones fell through, the slow ones collided" (P0) | Three causes, found by a headless repro that throws example 31's logs at a slab. **A body was moved a tick at a time and asked what it overlapped after**: at 56 m/s a log 0.7 m thick moves 0.93 m, and a terrain's collider is a shell of triangles with nothing behind it -- 4 of 4 went through at 56, 82 and 109 m/s, 0 of 4 up to 46. **Ground has a collider only near what moves, four chunks a tick**: a log thrown from out of reach arrived before its ground had been made. **And what gets behind the shell has nothing to push it out**: of 40 logs lying on ground raised and dug under them every tick, 6 ended inside or under it and 13 fell out of the bottom of the slab -- as did a log that struck end first and came round on that end, which a sweep of where a body goes does not see. | fixed | **Every dynamic body is swept** (`EMotionQuality::LinearCast`): Jolt sweeps a body only on a step that moves it more than three quarters of its inner radius, so a body at rest or at a walk costs what it did -- `physics1k`'s step is 0.63 ms where its baseline is 0.65. **The chunks a mover is about to cross are made the tick they are found missing**, whatever the tick's count: its own size, a tenth of a second of its travel and a metre (`Sweep`, terrain and block world both); still a function of the world (R10). **The ground is solid**: a loose body whose own middle is in a terrain's ground is put on top of it and stops falling -- asked of bodies that are moving, and of every loose body on a tick that remade a collider. The repro after: 0 through at every speed up to 440 m/s, logs and a 0.3 m ball, at the ground and at a plate 0.2 m thick; the edited ground 0 of 40 under it and 0 off the slab, and nothing moved faster than its own fall (17.9 m/s) -- a raise does not fling. Tests, failing first: `terrain_shape_tests.cpp`'s "a body thrown at the ground, or at a thin plate, stops on it at any speed", to 300 m/s; `physics_sync_tests.cpp`'s three D417 cases. **Two determinism traces moved, once, for this**: `ragdoll` and `terrain`, whose bodies are now swept; recorded again and reproduced. Not fixed: a long body spinning fast can still put an end through a PART thinner than its tip travels in a tick -- one tilted log of thirteen went through the 0.2 m plate, at 319 m/s; the sweep is linear, and a part has no "inside" to be put out of. **A character** the ground is raised round is put on top of it by the same rule, asked every tick (`physics_sync_tests.cpp`, "a character ground is raised round ends standing on it"). |

Test: `luau-check.sh` now looks for every `icons::` id the editor uses in the list as git has it STAGED -- the list a commit of the tree takes with it -- and fails naming the ones only an uncommitted list has; it failed on a probe naming `icons::ClassWater` and passes without. CI is the other half: red on `3ff55c15` at that line on Linux and Windows both. |
| D419 | the owner, 2026-10-01: paint looks like triangles and not like the brush -- a hard round brush left a polygon with triangular teeth, a stroke had scalloped edges and an unpainted speck inside it (P1) | Two causes. **The rim was a step**: a voxel was painted or it was not, by which side of the radius its middle fell, and the half-way line of what a mesh draws between such voxels is a staircase -- 0.44 of a voxel off the circle at its worst. **And a vertex painted wholly could draw as bare ground**: a triangle's three covers ride in one float, a byte each, and the shader takes them back by adding a half and truncating, which past 2^23 rounds to even -- a low byte of 255 became 256, nothing and a carry -- so a vertex of cover 255 in a triangle whose third corner was painted more than half showed the ground under it. That is the speck. | fixed | `paintBall` writes its rim as a ramp a voxel wide: how far inside the radius a voxel is, from whole half a voxel in to none half a voxel out, is how much of the paint may show there, in every mode (`Replace` lays the rim over what is there; `Erase` still takes everything inside the radius). A stroke's stamps move a voxel towards what its place on the rim allows and no further, so the edge is the envelope of the stamps. A vertex's cover is written as 254 at most (`MaxVertexCover`). Tests, failing first: `terrain_tests.cpp`'s "a hard round brush leaves a round edge, to a fraction of a voxel" (the edge within 0.2 of a voxel of the circle at 72 angles, where it was 0.44), "a stroke of a hard brush is whole inside its edge" and "a soft brush fades from its middle to its rim without a step back"; and the picture, drawn again. `BrushedDigest` and the `terrain` determinism trace moved for it, once: paint writes other covers at a rim. Not done: the shader's own rounding is as it was (nothing reaches it now), and paint finer than the voxels is not needed for a round edge at a metre voxel. |
| D420 | found by CI on `main`, 2026-10-02: `app` failed on one Windows shard at `field_streamer_tests.cpp`'s "held for the ground, the ring under the player is read at once", on a commit whose same tests had passed on `dev` an hour before (P2) | The test gave the pump a budget of fifty milliseconds and asserted the whole ring was in after it. The engine was right; the test asked a question of the machine's speed. | fixed | The test gives the pump the time to read the ring (a minute, of which it uses what the reads take): what it asserts is that ONE pump reads it, which is the claim. Not a test of its own: CI is, red on `main` at `fd37bec1` and green on `dev` at the same commit. | D421 | the owner, 2026-10-01: "I press play and click an instance by accident, and it is selected and opened in the workspace, when I am in play mode" -- and, the same day, a brush in hand whatever panel was on screen (P0) | **Play had no owner of the viewport.** Whether the game was running was asked at the sites somebody had remembered -- the Delete key, the menus -- and not at the rest: a click picked, selected and revealed what was behind the game's own button; a brush, the block, tile and water tools went on working on the running world; Ctrl+Z put an editing snapshot into it; drops and the interface handles worked; the wheel changed the editor's speed; Escape stopped the game the game was also given the key for; a key typed into the Console or a script moved the character, and a click on a panel was a click the game's scripts saw; F1 and Ctrl+P opened the editor's furniture over a game that uses them; a paused game kept a locked pointer; and Stop cleared the whole undo history. A tool, likewise, stayed in hand with its panel behind another tab ("open, not in front"), its ring following the pointer over every other panel's work. | fixed | **One answer, asked everywhere.** `Editor::viewportIsGames` (playing and not ejected) is what the pick, the tools, the event pump, the overlays, the drops, the wheel and the keys ask; `Editor::authoring` is what structure asks, and every authoring command is refused once, at the drain (`EditorCommands::refuseAuthoring`), whichever panel, key or menu asked -- `tools/repo/playlint.luau` fails the gate for a command that is in neither that list nor `keptInPlay`. The game hears a key only with the viewport focused and no editor field typed in, a press only over its picture or with the pointer it holds (`toViewportEvents`, `GameInput`), and what goes up always arrives. Escape is the game's; Stop is Shift+F5 and the transport; Shift+P ejects, which hands the pointer back, and a paused game holds none (`decidePointer`). The history is frozen while the game runs and is there again at Stop, with the unsaved mark as it was. The viewport wears a border while the game has it, and another while the editor is looking. **And `Editor::tool` is the tool in hand**: the one chosen while its panel is the one on screen and the scene is being edited, `Select` otherwise -- Terrain, Blocks, Tiles and Water by one rule; the activity bar's icon brings a panel behind another tab forward, where it closed it. Tests: `editor_tests.cpp` (the `D421:` cases -- a click in Play, a brush before Play, the history and the unsaved mark after Stop, the refused commands, who has the keyboard, what the game hears, every tool against its panel), `pointer_ownership_tests.cpp`. | `Editor::play` set a state and each caller decided what it meant. A rule about the whole viewport, held as checks at call sites, is held at the sites somebody thought of. |
| D422 | found making a real game for the owner, 2026-10-02: a folder of 235 trees and rocks, each a `Model` with attributes and a few plain anchored parts, on a large map. At run time every model was an empty shell, for ever; its parts stood in the world under `Workspace.Chunk_-1_-1_0` (P0) | **A streamed part came back under a folder of its cell, not under what it was authored under.** The partitioner leaves a `Nonatomic` model in the scene and files its parts by their own positions -- "empty, until the grid brings its parts back", its comment said -- and the glue that brought them back parented every record with no group to the cell's `Chunk_x_z_layer` folder. So `model:FindFirstChild("Trunk")` was nil, `model:GetChildren()` empty, and `model:Destroy()` left the tree drawn. The same was true of a part under a `Folder`, and of an atomic model, made in the cell's folder and not where it was. It needs a scene large enough to be cut into cells (four), which is why small tests kept their models whole. The oracle test compared the SET of parts and said in a comment that the tree's shape "differs by design". | fixed | **A record names its authored parent, and is born under it.** The partitioner marks each node a child leaves with a number (`"anchor"` in the scene it leaves behind); reading that scene records which instance each number became (`World::streamAnchor`), once, before any script runs -- so ninety-six models called `Tree` each get their own parts, whatever is added or destroyed afterwards. A cell's group carries the anchor (`ChunkGroup`, format 4; 3 and 2 still read): `existing` for an authored instance that stayed, whose records are born under it, or a `Model` made under the anchor for an atomic one. A group whose authored parent a script destroyed is not made again when its cell comes round: a felled tree does not grow back. The cell's folder is made only for records with no authored tree to return to -- a generated world's. A cached partition from an earlier build is not reused (`scene::PartitionRevision` in the cache's name). Tests: `partition_tests.cpp` -- "D422: a streamed part comes back under what it was authored under" (the game's shape: the whole tree, paths, classes, tags and attributes of what left and what stayed, equal to the unpartitioned scene's; out and in again; a destroyed model), and the cell format's older versions. | The test that would have caught it asserted the parts and excused the tree. What a script sees is the tree. |
| D423 | found making a game, 2026-10-02: six panes of glass at `Transparency` 0.25 to 0.9 on the engine default material -- two blended, the one at exactly 0.5 was stripes, and the three past it were not drawn at all (P0) | The shader clips at the material's alpha cutoff whatever its mode, and a block made from nothing began at glTF's default cutoff of a half: the plain part's block is made from nothing. An alpha test at 0.5 on a material nobody had called a mask. | fixed | A block has no cutoff unless the material is `Mask` (`GpuMaterialUniforms`): whoever means one writes it, as every `Mask` path already did. With it, the question the roadmap left open: **what is see-through casts no shadow** (`render::castsShadow`) -- a pane of glass and a part faded nearly away darkened the ground like a wall. Test: `render_world_tests.cpp`, "D423: a plain part faded past a half is still drawn"; pictured at all six levels. | A default that is right for one caller (a glTF that forgot to say) was the starting value of every other. |
| D424 | found making a game, 2026-10-02: every ball and cylinder a script made on the server was a block on a client -- the server printed `Enum.PartShape.Ball` and the client `Enum.PartShape.Block` for the same part (P0) | `Shape` was never in the wire schema: `BasePart` carries a part's place, size, body and surface, and what solid it is was nobody's field. A replica also collided with the wrong shape, so a body predicted against it was corrected every tick. | fixed | Protocol 30: a `Part` wire class with `Shape`, and a `MeshPart` one with `MeshContent`, `CollisionFidelity` and `MeshSize`, each extending `BasePart`. `Shape` is on `Part` because that is whose property it is -- and `wire_hash_tests` holds that what travels is what the world's hash sees change. Tests: `session_tests.cpp`, "D424: a part's shape reaches a replica"; run for real, a server and a client on port 7778. | The wire's list was checked for classes nobody decided about, and not for properties of a decided class that nobody sent. |
